\displaystyle=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\binom{n}{2}}\log_{2}\left(\frac{{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\epsilon}(n,m)\}}{{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\binom{n}{2}}\log_{2}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\epsilon}(n,m)|-r^{\ast}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{W\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\epsilon}}H(W)-r^{\ast}(\gamma)\,.

It suffices to show
r^{\ast}(\gamma)>\sup\{H(W):W\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\epsilon}\}=:I
. We always have
r^{\ast}(\gamma)\geqslant I
since
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\epsilon}\subseteq{\mathcal{X}}_{\gamma}
. If equality were to hold then
H(W)=r^{\ast}(\gamma)
for some
W\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\epsilon}
(since
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\epsilon}
is a compact set), which implies
W
is equivalent to a graphon in
{\mathcal{X}}^{\ast}_{\gamma}
(by
Proposition

3.1
), but this contradicts
\delta_{\square}(W,{\mathcal{X}}^{\ast}_{\gamma})\geqslant\epsilon>0
.
∎

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
p\in(0,1)
be a constant and let
G\sim G(n,p)
. For all
\epsilon>0
and large enough
n
,

{\mathbb{P}}\{\delta_{\square}(G,{\mathcal{X}}_{\ast}^{p})\geqslant\epsilon\,|\,G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\leqslant e^{-Cn^{2}}\,,

where
C>0
is a constant depending only on
\epsilon
and
p
.

Proof.

Fix
\epsilon>0
and define the set
{\mathcal{C}}^{\epsilon}(n):=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n):\delta_{\square}(G,{\mathcal{X}}_{\ast}^{p})\geqslant\epsilon\}
. Let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\epsilon}\subseteq{\mathcal{W}}
denote the set of graphons that are limits of sequences of graphs in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\epsilon}(n,m)
. Using
Theorem

1.2
, and applying
Lemma

2.9
to
{\mathcal{C}}^{\epsilon}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\binom{n}{2}}\log_{2}{\mathbb{P}}\{\delta_{\square}(G,{\mathcal{C}}_{\ast}^{p})\geqslant\epsilon\,|\,G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}

\displaystyle=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\binom{n}{2}}\log_{2}\left(\frac{{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\epsilon}(n)\}}{{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{\ast}(p)-\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\binom{n}{2}}\log_{2}{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\epsilon}(n)\}

\displaystyle\leqslant r_{\ast}(p)-\sup_{W\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\epsilon}}I_{p}(W)\,.

It suffices to show
r_{\ast}(p)<\sup\{I_{p}(W):W\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\epsilon}\}=:I
. We always have
r_{\ast}(p)\leqslant I
since
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\epsilon}\subseteq{\mathcal{X}}
. If equality were to hold then
I_{p}(W)=-r_{\ast}(p)
for some
W\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\epsilon}
(since
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\epsilon}
is a closed set), so
W\in{\mathcal{X}}_{\ast}^{p}
, but this contradicts
\delta_{\square}(W,{\mathcal{X}}_{\ast}^{p})\geqslant\epsilon>0
.
∎

4.
Exponential Penalties for Defect Edges

In this section we prove inequalities that will be used several times in
Sections

5
and
6
. Let
\epsilon\in(0,1/2^{64})
,
\alpha:=8\epsilon^{1/8}
, and
\eta:=\epsilon^{1/16}
. Recall that
V
denotes a vertex set of size
n
. Let
{\mathscr{B}}={\mathscr{B}}_{n}
denote the set of all bipartitions
V=A\cup B
such that

\left(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha\right)n\leqslant\min\{|A|\,,\,|B|\}\leqslant\max\{|A|\,,\,|B|\}\leqslant\left(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha\right)n\,.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\Pi=\{A,B\}\in{\mathscr{B}}
and fix an integer
0\leqslant m\leqslant|A|\cdot|B|
. Assume the following hypotheses:

(1)

Let
v\not\in V
be an additional vertex and let
N\subseteq A
be a subset of size
\alpha|A|\leqslant|N|\leqslant(1-\alpha)|A|
. Let
N^{\prime}\subseteq B
be a subset of size at least
\alpha|B|
. Let
F
denote the star centered at
v
with leaves
V\setminus(N\cup N^{\prime})
.

(2)

Let
T\subseteq\Pi^{c}
be a graph with at most
\epsilon n^{2}/\eta^{2}
edges.

(3)

Let
H\subseteq\Pi
be the uniformly random bipartite graph with
m
edges. Let
G
be the random graph on the vertex set
V\cup\{v\}
and edge set
E(H)\cup E(F)\cup(E(\Pi^{c})\setminus E(T))
.

For sufficiently large
n
,

{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n+1)\}\leqslant 4n\sqrt{p}\cdot e^{-\epsilon^{3/4}pn^{2}}\,,

where
p:=m/(|A|\cdot|B|)
.

Proof.

Let
H^{\prime}\subseteq\Pi
be the random bipartite graph obtained by including each edge independently with probability
p
, and let
G^{\prime}
be the random graph on the vertex set
V^{\prime}:=V\cup\{v\}
and edge set
E(H^{\prime})\cup E(F)\cup(E(\Pi^{c})\setminus E(T))
. Using
Lemma

A.4
, it is easily seen that

{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n+1)\}\leqslant 4n\sqrt{p}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\{G^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n+1)\}\,.

In the remainder, we will define a class of copies
K_{1,3}\subseteq K_{V^{\prime}}
and estimate the probability that
G^{\prime}
contains none of these copies as induced subgraphs. If
C\cong K_{1,3}
then let
c(C)
denote the vertex in
C
of degree three.

Definition and size of
{\mathscr{K}}
.

Let
N_{1}\subseteq N
and
N_{2}\subseteq A\setminus N
be subsets each of size at least
\alpha|A|
. Let
T^{\prime}:=(\Pi\cup T)^{c}
and notice that since
e(T)\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}/\eta^{2}
and
|A|,|B|\geqslant n/4
, we have

e_{T^{\prime}}(N_{1},N_{2})\geqslant(\alpha|A|)^{2}-\epsilon n^{2}/\eta^{2}\geqslant 2\epsilon^{1/4}n^{2}\,.

Let
J\subseteq K_{N_{1},N_{2}}
be a bipartite graph with at least
\epsilon^{1/4}n^{2}
edges, and denote the vertex sets
U_{A,1}:=V(J)\cap N_{1}
and
U_{A,2}:=V(J)\cap N_{2}
. Notice that since
|A|\geqslant n/4
, we have
|N^{\prime}|\geqslant\alpha n/4
.

Let
{\mathscr{K}}
denote the set of all subgraphs
C^{\prime}\subseteq K_{V^{\prime}}
isomorphic to
K_{1,3}
such that
V(C^{\prime})=\{v,w,x,y\}
,
x\in U_{A,1}
,
w=c(C^{\prime})\in U_{A,2}
, and
y\in N^{\prime}
. We have

|{\mathscr{K}}|=e(J)\cdot|N^{\prime}|\geqslant\epsilon^{1/4}n^{2}\cdot\frac{\alpha n}{4}=2\epsilon^{3/8}n^{3}\,.

For all
C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}
let
E_{C^{\prime}}
denote the event
\{G^{\prime}[V(C^{\prime})]=C^{\prime}\}
. Let
{\mathcal{I}}
be the lattice of events generated by
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}}
, i.e. by taking unions and intersections but not complements of the events
E_{C^{\prime}}
. For all distinct
C_{1},C_{2}\in{\mathscr{K}}
, the events
E_{C_{1}}
and
E_{C_{2}}
are not independent if and only if
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
share a vertex in
U_{A,1}\cup U_{A,2}
and in
N^{\prime}
, and in this case the probability both events
E_{C_{1}}
and
E_{C_{2}}
occur is
p^{3}
. We now calculate

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle:=\sum_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}}{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}\geqslant|{\mathscr{K}}|\cdot p^{2}\geqslant 2\epsilon^{3/8}p^{2}n^{3}\,,

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle:=\sum_{C_{1}\sim C_{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C_{1}}\wedge E_{C_{2}}\}\leqslant\sum_{w\in U_{A,1}\cup U_{A,2}}\binom{d_{J}(w)}{2}\cdot|N^{\prime}|\cdot p^{3}\leqslant p^{3}n^{4}\,,

where the sum defining
\Delta
is over unordered pairs
C_{1}\sim C_{2}
such that
E_{C_{1}}
and
E_{C_{2}}
are not independent. Since the family
{\mathcal{I}}
and the events
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}}\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}
satisfy the hypotheses of Janson’s inequality (
Theorem

2.1
), we obtain

{\mathbb{P}}\{G^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n+1)\}\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\bigwedge_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}}\overline{E_{C^{\prime}}}\right\}\leqslant\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{\mu}{2}\,,\,\frac{\mu^{2}}{4\Delta}\right\}\right)=e^{-\mu^{2}/4\Delta}\leqslant e^{-\epsilon^{3/4}pn^{2}}\,,

completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
n_{1},\,n_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\Pi_{1}=\{A_{1},B_{1}\}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{n_{1}}
, and
\Pi_{2}=\{A_{2},B_{2}\}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{n_{2}}
. Fix integers
0\leqslant m_{1}\leqslant|A_{1}|\cdot|B_{1}|
and
0\leqslant m_{2}\leqslant|A_{2}|\cdot|B_{2}|
. Let
n:=n_{1}+n_{2}
and
V=[n]
, and assume
n_{1}\geqslant\eta n
and
n_{2}\geqslant n_{1}/8
. Assume the following hypotheses:

(1)

Let
T\subseteq K_{V}\setminus(E(\Pi_{1})\cup E(\Pi_{2}))
be a graph with at most
\epsilon n^{2}/\eta^{2}
edges.

(2)

Assume there exists
v\in A_{2}
such that
d_{T}(v,A_{1})\geqslant\alpha|A_{1}|
and
d_{T}(v,B_{1})>(1-\alpha)|B_{1}|
.

(3)

For
i=1,2
, let
H_{i}\subseteq\Pi_{i}
be the uniformly random bipartite graph with
m_{i}
edges (here the graphs
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
are independent). Let
G
be the random graph on the vertex set
V
and edge set
(E(H_{1})\cup E(H_{2})\cup E(\Pi_{1}^{c})\cup E(\Pi_{2}^{c}))\,\Delta\,E(T)
, where
\Delta
is the symmetric difference.

For sufficiently large
n
,

{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\leqslant 8n^{2}\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha^{2}\eta p_{1}p_{2}}{2^{32}}\cdot n_{1}^{2}\right)\,,

where
p_{i}:=m_{i}/(|A_{i}|\cdot|B_{i}|)
for
i=1,2
.

Proof.

For
i=1,2
, let
H^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq\Pi_{i}
be the random bipartite graph obtained by including each edge independently with probability
p_{i}:=m_{i}/(|A_{i}|\cdot|B_{i}|)
. (The graphs
H^{\prime}_{1}
and
H^{\prime}_{2}
are assumed to be independent.) Let
G^{\prime}
be the random graph on the vertex set
V
and edge set
(E(H^{\prime}_{1})\cup E(H^{\prime}_{2})\cup E(\Pi_{1}^{c})\cup E(\Pi_{2}^{c}))\,\Delta\,E(T)
. Using
Lemma

A.4
and independence of the graphs
H^{\prime}_{i}
, it is easily seen that

{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\leqslant 8n^{2}\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\{G^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\,.

In the remainder, we will define a class of copies
K_{1,3}\subseteq K_{V}
and estimate the probability that
G^{\prime}
contains none of these copies as induced subgraphs. If
C\cong K_{1,3}
then let
c(C)
denote the vertex in
C
of degree three.

Definition and size of
{\mathscr{K}}
.

Let
N:=N_{T}(v,A_{1})
and
N^{\prime}:=N_{T}(v,B_{1})
. Since
\Pi_{1}\in{\mathscr{B}}_{n_{1}}
, we have
|A_{1}|,|B_{1}|\geqslant n_{1}/4
, so the hypotheses on
N
and
N^{\prime}
imply
|N|\geqslant\alpha n_{1}/4
and
|N^{\prime}|\geqslant n_{1}/8
.
Lemma

A.3
implies there exists a matching
M\subseteq K_{N,N^{\prime}}
with at least
\alpha n_{1}/2^{8}
edges. We claim there exists a subset
L\subseteq B_{2}
of size at least
|B_{2}|/2
such that for all
w\in L
we have

|\{e\in E(M):e\subseteq\overline{N}_{T}(w)\}|\geqslant\frac{\alpha n_{1}}{2^{9}}\,.

Indeed, if this were not the case, then since
|B_{2}|\geqslant n_{2}/4\geqslant n_{1}/32
, the induced bipartite graph
T[A_{1}\cup B_{1},L]
would contain at least
\alpha n_{1}^{2}/2^{15}\geqslant\alpha\eta^{2}n^{2}/2^{15}
edges, contradicting
e(T)\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}/\eta^{2}
. For all
w\in L
let

L_{w}:=\{e\in E(M):e\subseteq\overline{N}_{T}(w)\}\,,

which we have shown contains at least
\alpha n_{1}/2^{9}
edges. Let
{\mathscr{K}}
denote the set of all subgraphs
C^{\prime}\subseteq K_{V}
isomorphic to
K_{1,3}
such that, letting
V(C^{\prime})=\{v,w,x,y\}
,
c(C^{\prime})=v
,
w\in L
, and
xy\in L_{w}
. (To emphasize,
xy
is a
missing
edge in such a copy
C^{\prime}
.) Hence

|{\mathscr{K}}|=\sum_{w\in L}|L_{w}|\geqslant\frac{|B_{2}|}{2}\cdot\frac{\alpha n_{1}}{2^{9}}\geqslant\frac{\alpha n_{1}^{2}}{2^{15}}\,.

For all
C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}
let
E_{C^{\prime}}
be the event that
G^{\prime}[V(C^{\prime})]=C^{\prime}
and notice that
{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}=p_{1}p_{2}
. We compute that

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle:=\sum_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}}{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}=|{\mathscr{K}}|\cdot p_{1}p_{1}\geqslant\frac{\alpha n_{1}^{2}}{2^{15}}\cdot p_{1}p_{2}\,,

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle:=\sum_{C_{1}\sim C_{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C_{1}}\wedge E_{C_{2}}\}\leqslant e(M)\cdot|L|\cdot p_{1}p_{2}\leqslant p_{1}p_{2}\cdot n_{1}n_{2}\leqslant p_{1}p_{2}\cdot n_{1}^{2}/\eta\,,

where the sum defining
\Delta
is over unordered pairs
C_{1},C_{2}\in{\mathscr{K}}
such that
E_{C_{1}}
and
E_{C_{2}}
are not independent, and we used that
n_{2}\leqslant n_{1}/\eta
. Let
{\mathcal{I}}
be the lattice of events generated by
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}}
, i.e. by taking unions and intersections but not complements of the events
E_{C^{\prime}}
. Since the family
{\mathcal{I}}
and the events
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}}\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}
satisfy the hypotheses of Janson’s inequality (
Theorem

2.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\{G^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\bigwedge_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}}\{G^{\prime}[V(C^{\prime})]\neq C^{\prime}\}\right\}

\displaystyle\leqslant\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{\mu}{2}\,,\,\frac{\mu^{2}}{4\Delta}\right\}\right)\leqslant e^{-\mu^{2}/4\Delta}\leqslant\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha^{2}\eta p_{1}p_{2}}{2^{32}}\cdot n_{1}^{2}\right)\,,

completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\Pi=\{A,B\}\in{\mathscr{B}}
and fix integers
0\leqslant m\leqslant|A|\cdot|B|
,
0\leqslant d\leqslant\alpha n
,
0\leqslant k\leqslant n/8
,
0\leqslant l\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}
. Assume the following hypotheses:

(1)

Let
D\subseteq A
be a vertex subset of size
d
and let
D^{\prime}
be a set of size
d
that is disjoint from
V
. Let
M^{\prime}
be a perfect matching between
D
and
D^{\prime}
. Let
F^{\prime}\subseteq K_{D^{\prime},B}
be a bipartite graph such that
d_{F^{\prime}}(v,B)\leqslant\alpha|B|
for all
v\in D^{\prime}
.

(2)

Let
C
be a finite set of size
|C|\leqslant n/\eta
that is disjoint from
V\cup D^{\prime}
. Let
F\subseteq K_{V,C}
be a bipartite graph with
l
edges. Assume that for all
v\in C
,
d_{F}(v,A)<\alpha|A|
and
d_{F}(v,B)<\alpha|B|
.

(3)

Let
T\subseteq\Pi^{c}
be a graph with at most
\epsilon n^{2}/\eta^{2}
edges such that for all
v\in P\in\Pi
, we have
d_{T}(v)\leqslant\alpha|P|
. Assume
T
has a matching
M
covering
k
vertices.

(4)

Let
H\subseteq\Pi
be the uniformly random bipartite graph with
m
edges. Let
G
be the random graph on the vertex set
V\cup C\cup D
and edge set
E(H)\cup E(F)\cup E(M^{\prime})\cup(E(\Pi^{c})\setminus E(T))
.

Letting
n^{\prime}:=n+|C|+|D^{\prime}|
,

{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n^{\prime})\}\leqslant 4n\cdot p^{kn/2^{10}+dn/16}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{p^{4}}{2^{20}}\cdot n\cdot\min\{\eta^{2}l,\,n\}\right)\,,

where
p:=m/(|A|\cdot|B|)
.

Proof.

Let
H^{\prime}\subseteq\Pi
be the random bipartite graph obtained by including each edge independently with probability
p
, and let
G^{\prime}
be the random graph on the vertex set
V^{\prime}:=V\cup C\cup D^{\prime}
and edge set
E(H^{\prime})\cup E(F)\cup E(M^{\prime})\cup(E(\Pi^{c})\setminus E(T))
. Using
Lemma

A.4
, we see that

{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n^{\prime})\}\leqslant 4n\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\{G^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n^{\prime})\}\,.

In the remainder, we will define three classes of copies
K_{1,3}\subseteq K_{V^{\prime}}
and estimate the probability that
G^{\prime}
contains none of these copies as induced subgraphs. If
C\cong K_{1,3}
then let
c(C)
denote the vertex in
C
of degree three.

Definition and size of
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
.

Since
|V(M)|=k
, one of the two matchings
M[A]
or
M[B]
covers at least
k/2
vertices, so by symmetry we may assume
e(M[A])\geqslant k/4
. Let
X:=V(M[A])
denote the vertices covered by
M[A]
. Since
e(T)\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}
and
|B|\geqslant n/4
, we have

e(K_{B}\setminus T)\geqslant\binom{n/4}{2}-\frac{\epsilon n^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\geqslant\frac{n^{2}}{2^{7}}\,.

It follows from
Lemma

A.3
that
K_{B}\setminus T
has a matching
N
of size
n/2^{10}\leqslant e(N)\leqslant n/2^{9}
for large enough
n
. Let
Y\subseteq V(N)
be a set of vertices such that
|e\cap Y|=1
for all
e\in E(N)
.

Let
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
denote the set of all subgraphs
C^{\prime}\subseteq K_{V^{\prime}}
isomorphic to
K_{1,3}
such that
y:=c(C^{\prime})\in Y
and, if we denote
V(C^{\prime})=\{w,x,y,z\}
, then
wx\in E(M)
and
yz\in E(N)
. (To emphasize,
wx
is a
missing
edge in such
C^{\prime}
.) We have
|{\mathscr{K}}_{1}|=e(M[A])\cdot e(N)\geqslant kn/2^{12}
.

Definition and size of
{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
.

Since
v(N)\leqslant n/2^{8}
, there exists a subset
Z\subseteq B\setminus V(N)
of size
n/16\leqslant|Z|\leqslant n/8
. Let

Q\in\{D,X,A\setminus(X\cup D),V(N),Z,B\setminus(V(N)\cup Z)\}

be the vertex set such that
e_{F}(Q,C)
is maximum, and notice that
e_{F}(Q,C)\geqslant l/8
. Let
P\in\Pi
denote the part such that
Q\subseteq P
, and let
P^{\prime}\in\Pi\setminus\{P\}
denote the other part. Define the set

R:=\begin{cases}X\cup D&\text{if }Q=A\setminus(X\cup D)\\
A\setminus(X\cup D)&\text{if }Q\in\{X,D\}\\
V(N)\cup Z&\text{if }Q=B\setminus(V(N)\cup Z)\\
B\setminus(V(N)\cup Z)&\text{if }Q\in\{V(N),Z\}\end{cases}

and the set
S:=P^{\prime}\setminus(X\cup V(N)\cup D\cup Z)
. For all
v\in C
define the set
U_{v}:=\overline{N}_{F}(v,P^{\prime})
, which has size at least
(1-\alpha)|P^{\prime}|
by hypothesis. For all
v\in C
define the set
K_{v}:=U_{v}\cap S
. For all
wx\in E_{F}(Q,C)
with
w\in C
and
x\in Q
, let
L_{wx}:=\overline{N}_{F}(w,R)\cap\overline{N}_{T}(x,R)
.

Let
{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
denote the set of all subgraphs
C^{\prime}\subseteq K_{V^{\prime}}
isomorphic to
K_{1,3}
such that, if we denote
V(C^{\prime})=\{w,x,y,z\}
, then
wx\in E_{F}(Q,C)
,
w\in C
,
x=c(C^{\prime})\in Q
,
y\in L_{wx}
, and
z\in K_{w}
. We will now estimate the size of
{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
.

Since
|X|\leqslant n/8
,
|D|\leqslant\alpha n
, and
|V(N)|\leqslant n/2^{9}
, and
|Z|\leqslant n/8
, we have

\displaystyle|S|

\displaystyle=|P^{\prime}\setminus(X\cup V(N)\cup D\cup Z)|

\displaystyle\geqslant|P^{\prime}|-|P^{\prime}\cap(X\cup V(N)\cup D\cup Z)|

\displaystyle\geqslant\left(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha\right)n-\max\{|X|+|D|,|V(N)|+|Z|\}\geqslant\frac{7n}{16}-\frac{3n}{16}=\frac{n}{4}\,,

and the same calculation proves
|R|\geqslant n/4
. Notice that for all
v\in C
,
K_{v}=S\setminus(P^{\prime}\setminus U_{v})
, hence the inequalities
|P^{\prime}\setminus U_{v}|\leqslant\alpha|P^{\prime}|
and
\alpha<1/16
imply

(20)

|K_{v}|\geqslant|S|-|P^{\prime}\setminus U_{v}|\geqslant\frac{n}{4}-\alpha|P^{\prime}|\geqslant\frac{n}{8}\,.

Using the fact that
d_{F}(w,P)\leqslant\alpha|P|
and
d_{T}(x,P)\leqslant\alpha|P|
and
\alpha<1/32
, we compute

|L_{wx}|\geqslant|R|-d_{F}(w,P)-d_{T}(x,P)\geqslant\frac{n}{4}-2\alpha|P|\geqslant\frac{n}{8}\,.

We compute that

|{\mathscr{K}}_{2}|\geqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}wx\in E_{F}(Q,C)\\
w\in C,\,x\in Q\end{subarray}}|K_{w}|\cdot|L_{wx}|\geqslant\frac{n^{2}}{64}\cdot e_{F}(Q,C)\geqslant\frac{ln^{2}}{2^{9}}\,,

where we used that
e_{F}(Q,C)\geqslant l/8
.

Definition and size of
{\mathscr{K}}_{3}
.

Since
d=|D|\leqslant\alpha n
and
d_{T}(v)\leqslant\alpha|P|
for all
v\in V
and
P\in\Pi
, there exists a subset
D^{\prime\prime}\subseteq P\setminus D
such that the induced bipartite graph
(\Pi\cup T)^{c}[D,D^{\prime\prime}]
contains a perfect matching
J
between
D
and
D^{\prime\prime}
. For all
y\in D^{\prime}
let
Z_{y}:=Z\cap\overline{N}_{F^{\prime}}(y,B)
. Since
d_{F^{\prime}}(v,B)\leqslant\alpha|B|
, we have

|Z_{y}|\geqslant|Z|-d_{F^{\prime}}(v,B)\geqslant\frac{n}{16}-\frac{\alpha n}{4}\geqslant\frac{n}{32}\,.

Let
{\mathscr{K}}_{3}
be the set of all subgraphs
C^{\prime}\subseteq K_{V^{\prime}}
isomorphic to
K_{1,3}
such that, if we denote
V(C^{\prime})=\{w,x,y,z\}
, then
w=c(C^{\prime})\in D
,
wx\in E(J)
,
y\in D^{\prime}
,
wy\in E(M^{\prime})
, and
z\in Z_{y}
. It follows directly from the definitions that

|{\mathscr{K}}_{3}|=\sum_{w\in D}|Z_{y}|\geqslant\frac{dn}{32}\,.

With the definitions of
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
,
{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
, and
{\mathscr{K}}_{3}
, we now analyze the probability
G^{\prime}
contains no induced copy
C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{1}\cup{\mathscr{K}}_{2}\cup{\mathscr{K}}_{3}
. For all
C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{1}\cup{\mathscr{K}}_{2}\cup{\mathscr{K}}_{3}
let
E_{C^{\prime}}
denote the event
\{G^{\prime}[V(C^{\prime})]=C^{\prime}\}
. First consider copies in
{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
. Let
{\mathcal{I}}
be the lattice of events generated by
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{2}}
, i.e. by taking unions and intersections but not complements of the events
E_{C^{\prime}}
. The events
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{2}}
are not independent, but the family
{\mathcal{I}}
and the events
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{2}}
satisfy the hypotheses of Janson’s inequality (
Theorem

2.1
). Since
{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}=p^{2}
for all
C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
, we compute

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle:=\sum_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{2}}{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}=|{\mathscr{K}}_{2}|\cdot p^{2}\geqslant\frac{p^{2}ln^{2}}{2^{9}}\,,

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C_{1},C_{2}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{2}\\
C_{1}\sim C_{2}\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C_{1}}\wedge E_{C_{2}}\}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}wx\in E_{F}(Q,C)\\
w\in C,x\in Q\end{subarray}}\binom{d_{F}(x,C)}{2}\left(|L_{wx}|+|K_{w}|\right)+|A|\cdot|B|\cdot\binom{e(F)}{2}\leqslant\frac{l\cdot|C|^{2}}{2}\cdot n+\frac{l^{2}n^{2}}{4}\,,

where the sum defining
\Delta
is over unordered pairs
C_{1},C_{2}
such that
E_{C_{1}}
and
E_{C_{2}}
are not independent. To calculate
\Delta
, we observed that if
E_{C_{1}}
and
E_{C_{2}}
are not independent then at least one of the following cases holds (letting
V(C_{i})=\{w_{i},x_{i},y_{i},z_{i}\}
for
i=1,2
):

(1)

x_{1}=x_{2}\in Q
and
z_{1}=z_{2}\in S
. In this case
E_{C_{1}}
and
E_{C_{2}}
are not independent since they share the edge
x_{1}z_{1}=x_{2}z_{2}
. There are at most
l\cdot|C|^{2}n/2
such pairs of events.

(2)

y_{1}=y_{2}\in R
and
z_{1}=z_{2}\in S
. In this case
E_{C_{1}}
and
E_{C_{2}}
are not independent since they share the edge
y_{1}z_{1}=y_{2}z_{2}
. There are at most
l^{2}n^{2}/4
such pairs of events.

Since
|C|\leqslant n/\eta
, we compute that

\frac{\mu^{2}}{4\Delta}\geqslant\frac{p^{4}l^{2}n^{4}/2^{18}}{2l\cdot n^{3}/\eta^{2}+l^{2}n^{2}}\geqslant\frac{p^{4}ln^{2}}{2^{20}\cdot\max\{n/\eta^{2},\,l\}}=\frac{p^{4}}{2^{20}}\cdot n\cdot\min\{\eta^{2}l,\,n\}\,.

Now consider the copies in
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}\cup{\mathscr{K}}_{3}
. By definition of
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
, the events
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{1}}
are independent, and we have
{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}=p^{4}
for all
C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
. Similarly, the events
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{3}}
are independent, and we have
{\mathbb{P}}\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}=p^{2}
for all
C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{3}
. Additionally, the three collections of events
\{E_{C^{\prime}}\}_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{i}}
for
i=1,2,3
are pairwise independent as collections. We thus compute that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\{G^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\bigwedge_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{1}\cup{\mathscr{K}}_{2}\cup{\mathscr{K}}_{3}}\overline{E_{C^{\prime}}}\right\}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\left\{\bigwedge_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{1}}\overline{E_{C^{\prime}}}\right\}{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\bigwedge_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{2}}\overline{E_{C^{\prime}}}\right\}{\mathbb{P}}\left\{\bigwedge_{C^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{K}}_{3}}\overline{E_{C^{\prime}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leqslant p^{4|{\mathscr{K}}_{1}|+2|{\mathscr{K}}_{3}|}\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{\mu}{2}\,,\,\frac{\mu^{2}}{4\Delta}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=p^{4|{\mathscr{K}}_{1}|+2|{\mathscr{K}}_{3}|}\cdot e^{-\mu^{2}/4\Delta}

\displaystyle\leqslant p^{kn/2^{10}+dn/16}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{p^{4}}{2^{20}}\cdot n\cdot\min\{\eta^{2}l,\,n\}\right)\,,

which completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

5.
The Supercritical Regime

In this section we prove the first and second assertions of
Theorem

1.5
. Throughout the section, let
\gamma\in\big(\frac{5-\sqrt{5}}{4},1\big)
,
m\sim\gamma\binom{n}{2}
, and
\mu:=\big(\frac{5+\sqrt{5}}{10}\gamma\big)^{1/2}
. There is a global parameter
\epsilon>0
used throughout the section; in general, all statements hold for sufficiently small
\epsilon
, but in some cases it will be useful to have established that
0<\epsilon<\min\{\gamma-\mu,1-\gamma\}/2^{64}
. Likewise, inequalities
f(n)\leqslant g(n)
are always meant for large enough
n
. We examine typical structure at the critical edge density at the end of the section.

5.1.
Parameters and Definitions

Define the parameters and constant

\alpha:=8\epsilon^{1/8}\,,\hskip 22.76219pt\delta:=8\sqrt{\epsilon}\,,\hskip 22.76219pt\tau:=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2^{8}}\right)^{67}\,,\hskip 22.76219pt\rho:=2\gamma-1\,.

Let
W^{\ast}=f^{\ast}_{\gamma}
be the graphon defined in
(
8
)
, so
W^{\ast}
is the unique optimal graphon (up to equivalence) in the supercritical regime. Define the set of graphs

{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathsf{far}}:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m):\delta_{\square}(G,W^{\ast})\geqslant\tau\}\,.

For disjoint sets
A,B,C\subseteq V
with
A
and
B
nonempty such that
V=A\cup B\cup C
, call
\Pi=\{\{A,B\},C\}
a
division
of
V
. For a division
\Pi
, let
\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}:=\{A,B\}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}:=C
(thought of as the “co-bipartite” and “sparse” parts of
\Pi
, respectively). Let
{\mathscr{D}}
denote the set of all divisions of
V
, define the set of
balanced
divisions

{\mathscr{P}}:=\left\{\Pi\in{\mathscr{D}}:\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)n\leqslant\min\{|P|:P\in\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}\}\leqslant\max\{|P|:P\in\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}\}\leqslant\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)n\right\}\,.

For all
s\geqslant 0
define the set of divisions

{\mathscr{P}}_{s}:=\{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}:|\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}|=s\}\,.

and define the set of bipartitions
{\mathscr{B}}:=\{\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}:\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{0}\}
. For all
G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)
and
\Pi=\{\{A,B\},C\}\in{\mathscr{D}}
let

b(G,\Pi):=e(G^{c}[A])+e(G^{c}[B])+e(G[V,C])\,,

let
\Pi(G)\in{\mathscr{D}}
denote a canonically chosen division minimizing
b(G,\Pi)
, and define
b(G):=b(G,\Pi(G))
. For all
G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)
let
\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}(G):=(\Pi(G))_{\mathsf{cb}}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}(G):=(\Pi(G))_{\mathsf{sp}}
. Throughout this section, we often write
\Pi
to mean the complete bipartite graph
K_{\Pi}
when there is no ambiguity. For all
G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)
with
\Pi(G)=\{\{A,B\},C\}
, define

D(G):=E(G^{c}[A])\cup E(G^{c}[B])\cup E(G[A\cup B,C])\,,

which we view as “defect” edges. Let
U(G):=V(D(G))
denote the set of vertices incident to
D(G)
and define the graph
T(G):=(U(G),D(G))
. For all
\Pi=\{\{A,B\},C\}\in{\mathscr{P}}
define

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathsf{far}}:\Pi(G)=\Pi\,,\,D(G)\neq\emptyset\}\,,\\[5.0pt]
{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathsf{far}}:\Pi(G)=\Pi\,,\,D(G)=\emptyset\}\,,\end{array}

and define the associated sets of graphs

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}&:=\{T(G)\cup G[C]:G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi}\}\,,\\[5.0pt]
{\mathcal{T}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}&:=\{G[C]:G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}\}\,.\end{array}

For all
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}
and
P\in\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}=\{A,B\}
, say that a vertex
v
has

(1)

low degree in
P
if
d_{T}(v,P)<\alpha|P|
,

(2)

medium degree in
P
if
\alpha|P|\leqslant d_{T}(v,P)\leqslant(1-\alpha)|P|
, or

(3)

high degree in
P
if
d_{T}(v,P)>(1-\alpha)|P|
.

If a vertex
v
has medium degree in some
P\in\{A,B\}
with respect to
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}
, we say that
v
has
(\Pi,T)
-medium degree, or simply medium degree. For all
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}
and
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}
let

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi}:\text{there exists a vertex of $(\Pi,T(G))$-medium degree}\}\,,\\[5.0pt]
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}:T(G)\cup G[C]=T\}\,.\end{array}

Our first lemma toward proving
Theorem

1.5

1
describes some useful properties of graphs that are close to
W^{\ast}
in cut metric.

Lemma 5.1
.

For large enough
n
, every graph
G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathsf{far}}
satisfies the following conditions:

(1)

By editing (i.e. adding or removing) at most
\epsilon n^{2}
edges of
G
, we can obtain a co-bipartite graph.

(2)

\Pi(G)\in{\mathscr{P}}
and
|\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}(G)|\leqslant\delta n/2
.

(3)

|\rho-d_{G}(A,B)|\leqslant\delta
.

(4)

For all
P\in\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}(G)
and
v\in P
it holds that
d_{T(G)}\leqslant(1-\alpha)|P|
.

It follows immediately from
Lemma

5.1
and the above definitions that

(21)

|{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)|\leqslant|{\mathcal{B}}_{c}(n,m)|+\sum_{s=1}^{\delta n}\,\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|+\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}}\Bigg(\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}}|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}|+|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}|\Bigg)+|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathsf{far}}|\,.

Thus to prove
Theorem

1.5

1
, it suffices to show that the three rightmost terms in
(
21
)
are much smaller than
|{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)|
, which is the focus of the remainder of this section. The proof of
Lemma

5.1
uses the following simple object.

Definition 5.2

(Discretization of a graphon)
.

For all
W\in{\mathcal{W}}
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, let
H_{n}
be the weighted graph on
V
whose
ij
edge weight is
W(\frac{i}{n},\frac{j}{n})
and whose node-weights all equal 1. Define the
n
th discretization of
W
to be the graphon
W_{n}:=W_{H_{n}}
(recall the definition of
W_{H}
for a weighted graph
H
given in
Section

2.4
).

Proof of
Lemma

5.1
.

Denote
W:=W^{\ast}
. For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, let
H_{n}
be the weighted graph on
V
whose
ij
edge weight is
W(\frac{i}{n},\frac{j}{n})
and whose node-weights all equal 1. In the remainder, all statements hold for large enough
n
. The sequence
W_{n}
clearly converges to
W
pointwise almost everywhere and hence in cut metric, so the condition
\delta_{\square}(G,W)<\tau
implies
\delta_{\square}(G,W_{n})<2\tau
. By
[
41
, Lemma 8.9]
we have
\delta_{\square}(G,H_{n})=\delta_{\square}(W_{G},W_{n})
, and by
[
15
, Theorem 2.3]
we have

\widehat{\delta}(G_{1},G_{2})\leqslant 32\cdot(\delta_{\square}(G_{1},G_{2}))^{1/67}

for edge-weighted graphs with weights in
[-1,1]
; from these two results and our definition of
\tau
we deduce that
\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(G,H_{n})<\epsilon/8
. Since the vertex set of
H_{n}
can be partitioned into two parts within each of which all edges have weight 1 (we may disregard the one or two isolated vertices of
H_{n}
), it follows that there is a bipartition
\Pi^{\prime}=\{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}\}
of
V(G)
such that each of the graphs
G^{c}[A^{\prime}]
and
G^{c}[B^{\prime}]
has at most
\epsilon n^{2}/8
non-edges. Hence by adding at most
\epsilon n^{2}/4
edges to
G[A^{\prime}]
and
G[B^{\prime}]
, we can obtain a co-bipartite graph (and we made a total of less than
\epsilon n^{2}
edits), completing the proof of
1
.

To prove
2
, first let
\Pi(G)=\{\{A,B\},C\}
and note that since
G
is within
\epsilon n^{2}
in edit distance of
{\mathcal{B}}_{c}(n,m)
by part (
i
), we have
b(G)\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}
. Let
H
be the graph on
n
vertices with edge set
E(K_{A})\cup E(K_{B})\cup E(G[A,B])
. If
|C|>\delta n/2
then
\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(H,H_{n})\geqslant\rho\frac{\delta}{2}(1-\frac{\delta}{2})
, which implies

\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(G,H_{n})\geqslant\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(H,H_{n})-\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(G,H)\geqslant{\textstyle\rho\frac{\delta}{2}(1-\frac{\delta}{2})}-\epsilon\geqslant\frac{\epsilon}{8}\,,

but this contradicts the opposite inequality in the previous paragraph, so we must have
|C|\leqslant\delta n/2
. If
|A|<(\frac{1}{2}-\delta)n
then since
|C|\leqslant\delta n/2
, we have
|B|\geqslant(\frac{1}{2}+\delta/2)n
. It follows that

\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(H,H_{n})\geqslant\frac{\delta}{4}(1-\rho)>\sqrt{\epsilon}\,,

which contradicts the fact that

\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(H,H_{n})\leqslant\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(H,G)+\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(G,H_{n})\leqslant\epsilon+\frac{\epsilon}{8}\,,

proving
|A|,|B|\geqslant(\frac{1}{2}-\delta)n
. The same argument clearly shows
|A|,|B|\leqslant(\frac{1}{2}+\delta)n
.

The third assertion of the lemma follows from a case analysis that is proven in greater generality in
Lemma

6.2

3
, so we refer the reader to that proof.

To prove
4
, suppose a vertex
v\in A
satisfies
d_{T(G)}(v,A)>(1-\alpha)|A|
. By optimality of
\Pi(G)
, we know
\overline{d}_{G}(v,B)\geqslant\overline{d}_{G}(v,A)
, hence

\displaystyle\overline{d}_{G}(v)

\displaystyle=\overline{d}_{G}(v,A)+\overline{d}_{G}(v,B)+\overline{d}_{G}(v,C)\geqslant 2(1-\alpha)\big(\textstyle{\frac{1}{2}}-\delta\big)n\geqslant(1-4\alpha)n

and
d_{G}(v)\leqslant 4\alpha n
. If we define
\Pi^{\prime}:=\{\{A\setminus\{v\},B\},C\cup\{v\}\}
, then since
(1-\alpha)|A|\geqslant n/4
,

\displaystyle b(G,\Pi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leqslant b(G,\Pi(G))+d_{G}(v)-d_{T(G)}(v,A)

\displaystyle\leqslant b(G,\Pi(G))+4\alpha n-(1-\alpha)|A|\leqslant b(G,\Pi)-({\textstyle\frac{1}{4}}-4\alpha)n<b(G,\Pi(G))\,,

which contradicts optimality of
\Pi(G)
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

For all
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}
we have

|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}|\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})}e^{-\beta n^{2}}\,,

where
\beta=\beta(\epsilon)>0
is a constant.

Proof.

Denote
\Pi=\{\{A,B\},C\}
. For all
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}
and all
v\in V
, define the sets

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}^{\prime}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi}^{\prime}:T(G)\cup G[C]=T\}\,,\\[5.0pt]
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T,v}^{\prime}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}^{\prime}:v\text{ has $(\Pi,T(G))$-medium degree}\}\,.\end{array}

Fix
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}
and
v\in V
such that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T,v}^{\prime}
is nonempty. For convenience we may assume
v\in A\cup C
. By optimality of
\Pi(G)
, every graph
G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi,T,v}
satisfies
\overline{d}_{B}(v)\geqslant\alpha|A|
(since otherwise we could obtain a more optimal partition by moving
v
to
B
). Let
N\subseteq A
be a subset of size
\alpha|A|\leqslant|N|\leqslant(1-\alpha)|A|
, and let
N^{\prime}\subseteq B
be a subset of size at least
\alpha|A|
. The sets
N
and
N^{\prime}
represent the vertices in
A
and
B
, respectively, to which
v
is non-adjacent. Let
A^{\prime}:=A\setminus\{v\}
and let
H
be the uniformly random subgraph of
K_{A^{\prime},B}
with exactly

m^{\prime}:=m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})+e(T[A\cup B])-e(T[V,C])

edges. Let
G
be the random graph
H\cup(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c}\setminus T)
. Now by applying
Lemma

4.1
with the bipartition
\{A^{\prime},B\}
, defect graph
T[A^{\prime}\cup B]
, the sets
N
and
N^{\prime}
, and the random graph
H
, we find that

{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\leqslant e^{-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{3/4}pn^{2}}

where
p:=m^{\prime}/(|A^{\prime}|\cdot|B|)
. Since every graph in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T,v}^{\prime}
is of the form
H^{\prime}\cup(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c}\setminus T)\cup T[V,C]
for some bipartite graph
H^{\prime}\subseteq K_{A^{\prime},B}
, since there are at most
2^{2n}
choices for the sets
N
and
N^{\prime}
, and since we assumed
v\in A\cup C
, we have

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T,v}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2^{2n+1}\cdot\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m^{\prime}}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2^{2n+1}\cdot\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m^{\prime}}\cdot e^{-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{3/4}pn^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})}\exp\left(\left|1-\frac{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})-(m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c}))}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})}\right|\epsilon n^{2}\right)\cdot 2^{2n+1}\cdot e^{-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{3/4}pn^{2}}

(22)

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})}e^{\epsilon n^{2}}\cdot 2^{2n+1}\cdot e^{-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{3/4}pn^{2}}\,,

where the third inequality is an application of
Lemma

A.1
using
e(T)\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}
, and the last inequality holds since we can apply
Lemma

5.1
(
ii
) to compute

0<\frac{\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)^{2}}{(\gamma+\epsilon)\binom{n}{2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\right)^{2}}-1\leqslant\frac{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})-(m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c}))}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})}\leqslant\frac{\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)^{2}}{(\gamma-\epsilon)\binom{n}{2}-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\right)^{2}}-1<2\,,

which holds provided
n
is large enough that
(\gamma-\epsilon)\binom{n}{2}\leqslant m\leqslant(\gamma+\epsilon)\binom{n}{2}
. Using
(
22
)
, we compute

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{t=1}^{\epsilon n^{2}}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}\\
e(T)=t\end{subarray}}\,\sum_{v\in V}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi,T,v}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}\cdot\binom{\binom{n}{2}}{\epsilon n^{2}}\cdot n\cdot\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})}e^{\epsilon n^{2}}\cdot 2^{2n+1}\cdot e^{-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{3/4}pn^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{3/4}pn^{2}}\cdot\epsilon n^{3}\cdot\left(\frac{e}{\epsilon}\right)^{\epsilon n^{2}}\cdot 2^{2n+1}\,.

Since
0<\epsilon<1/2^{64}
and
p>1/4
, it is easy to see that
\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{3/4}p>\epsilon\left(1+\log\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)\right)
, which implies the claimed inequality for a constant
\beta=\beta(\epsilon)>0
.
∎

Define the set of graphs

{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}:=\{T[G]\cup G[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}]:G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}\}\,.

For all
\Pi=\{\{A,B\},C\}\in{\mathscr{P}}
and
T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}
, let
M(T)
be a canonically chosen maximum matching of the graph
T[A\cup B]
. Let
X(T):=V(M(T))
denote the set of vertices covered by the matching
M(T)
. For all
k,l\geqslant 0
define the sets of graphs

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{cb}}_{\Pi,k}&:=\left\{T[A\cup B]:T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}\,,\,|X(T)|=k\right\}\,,\\[10.0pt]
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{btw}}_{\Pi,l}&:=\left\{T[A\cup B,C]:T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}\,,\,e(T[A\cup B,C])=l\right\}\,,\\[10.0pt]
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{sp}}_{\Pi}&:=\{T[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}]:T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}\}\,,\\[10.0pt]
{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi,k,l}&:=\left\{T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}:T[A\cup B]\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{cb}}_{\Pi,k}\,,\,T[A\cup B,C]\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{btw}}_{\Pi,l}\right\}\,.\end{array}

Lemma 5.4
.

For all
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}
,
k\geqslant 2
,
l\geqslant 1
, and
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi,k,l}
, we have

|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}|\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})-e(T[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}])}e^{-\xi kn-\zeta n\cdot\min\{l,n\}}

where
\xi,\zeta>0
are constants depending only on
\epsilon
.

Proof.

Denote
\Pi=\{\{A,B\},C\}
. Let
H
be the uniformly random subgraph of
\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}
with exactly

m^{\prime}:=m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})+e(T[A\cup B])-e(T[A\cup B,C])-e(T[C])

edges, and define
p:=m^{\prime}/(|A|\cdot|B|)
. Let
G
be the random graph
H\cup(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c}\setminus T)\cup T[V,C]
. We now apply
Lemma

4.3
with the following variable assignments

\begin{array}[]{lllll}\Pi\leftarrow\Pi_{i}&m\leftarrow m^{\prime}&D\leftarrow\emptyset&D^{\prime}\leftarrow\emptyset&F^{\prime}\leftarrow\emptyset\\[10.0pt]
k\leftarrow k&d\leftarrow 0&l\leftarrow l&C\leftarrow C&F\leftarrow T[A\cup B,V\setminus(A\cup B)]\,.\end{array}

The hypotheses of
Lemma

4.3
are indeed met because
T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}
implies there is no medium degree vertex, and
Lemma

5.1

4
implies every vertex
v\in P\in\Pi
satisfies
d_{T}(v,P)<\alpha|P|
. Hence

{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\leqslant p^{kn/2^{11}}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{p^{4}}{2^{25}}\min\left\{ln,n^{2}\right\}\right)

(notice we have divided by two in the exponents of the right-hand side to account for the fact that
|A\cup B|\geqslant(1-\delta)n
). Since every graph in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}
is of the form
H^{\prime}\cup(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c}\setminus T)\cup T[V,C]
for some bipartite graph
H^{\prime}\subseteq\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}
, we have

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m^{\prime}}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})-e(T[C])}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})-(m-e(\Pi^{c}_{\mathsf{cb}}))}{m-e(\Pi^{c}_{\mathsf{cb}})}\right)(2\alpha kn+l)\right)\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})-e(T[C])}e^{2\alpha kn+l}\cdot p^{kn/2^{11}}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{p^{4}}{2^{25}}\min\left\{ln,n^{2}\right\}\right)\,,

for all sufficiently large
n
, where the second inequality follows by using
Lemma

A.1
and noticing that
e(T[A\cup B])\leqslant 2\alpha kn
(since by definition of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}
, the set
A\cup B
only has vertices that have low degree in their respective parts, and every edge of
T[A\cup B]
is incident to
X(T)
since the matching
M(T)
is maximum). The third inequality holds for the same reasons
(
22
)
was shown to hold. Since
p\geqslant 1/4
is of constant order, from the last inequality above, we see that the claimed inequality holds for small enough
\epsilon>0
and constants
\xi,\zeta>0
.
∎

For all
s\geqslant 1
, let
{\mathscr{P}}_{s}
denote the set of all
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}
such that
|\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}|=s
. For all
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}
, let
\Pi_{\ast}
denote either
\{A\cup\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}},B\}
or
\{A,B\cup\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}\}
, canonically chosen.

Lemma 5.5
.

If
\gamma\in\big(\frac{5-\sqrt{5}}{4},1\big)
then for all
s\geqslant 1
and all
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}
we have

|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi}^{\ast}|\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\ast})}{m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})}e^{-\nu sn}\,,

where
\nu>0
is a constant depending only on
\gamma.

Proof.

Denote
\Pi=\{\{A,B\},C\}
, and we may assume
\Pi_{\ast}=\{A\cup C,B\}
. For all
T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}
let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi,T}
denote the set of all
G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}
such that
T(G)=T
. Define the quantities
a:=|A|
,
b:=|B|
,
N:=e(\Pi_{\ast})
,
M:=m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})
, and
t:=e(T)
. Since
\gamma>\frac{5-\sqrt{5}}{4}
we have
(1-\rho)^{2}/\rho<1
, so by
Lemma

5.1

3
, there is a constant
\nu^{\prime}>0
depending only on
\gamma
such that
(N-M)^{2}/(NM)<e^{-\nu^{\prime}}
for small enough
\epsilon
and large enough
n
. Similarly, we have
(N-M)/M<e
for large enough
n
. Hence for all
T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}
, we use
Lemma

A.1
to bound

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi,T}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})-e(T)}

\displaystyle=\binom{e(\Pi_{\ast})-sb}{m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})+sa+\binom{s}{2}-e(T)}

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\ast})}{m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})}\left(\frac{N-M}{N}\right)^{sb}\left(\frac{N-M}{M}\right)^{sa+\binom{s}{2}-e(T)}

\displaystyle=\binom{e(\Pi_{\ast})}{m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})}\left(\frac{(N-M)^{2}}{NM}\right)^{sb}\left(\frac{N-M}{M}\right)^{s(a-b)+\binom{s}{2}-e(T)}

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\ast})}{m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})}e^{-\nu^{\prime}sb+s(a-b)+s^{2}}\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\ast})}{m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})}e^{-\nu^{\prime\prime}sn}\,,

which holds for
\nu^{\prime\prime}:=\nu^{\prime}/8
and sufficiently large
n
; we have also used the fact that
\nu^{\prime}b>|a-b|
for small enough
\epsilon>0
since
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}
and
|a-b|\leqslant 2\delta
. Since
|{\mathcal{T}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|\leqslant 2^{\binom{s}{2}}
,

|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|=\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi,T}|\leqslant 2^{\binom{s}{2}}\binom{e(\Pi_{\ast})}{m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})}e^{-\nu^{\prime\prime}sn}\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\ast})}{m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})}e^{-\nu sn}\,,

where
\nu:=\nu^{\prime\prime}/2
, completing the proof.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

1.5

1
.

First, for all
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}
,
Lemma

5.3
shows

(23)

|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}|\leqslant\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})}e^{-\beta n^{2}}\leqslant e^{-\beta n^{2}/2}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|\,,

where the second inequality is immediate from
Corollary

C.3.1
, which shows almost all co-bipartite graphs with edge density
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
admit a unique 2-clique-cover.

We now obtain a bound on
\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}}|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}|
for
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}
and
k\geqslant 2
. First notice that for all
T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{cb}}_{\Pi,k}
, every vertex
v\in X(T)
satisfies
d_{T}(v,P)<\alpha|P|
for
P\in\{A,B\}
, which follows from
Lemma

5.1

4
and the definition of
{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{cb}}_{\Pi,k}
. Since every edge in
T[A\cup B]
is incident to
X(T)
, we have

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{cb}}_{\Pi,k}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{X\in\binom{V}{k}}\,\prod_{P\in\Pi}\,\prod_{v\in P\cap X}\binom{|P|}{\leqslant\alpha|P|}\leqslant\sum_{X\in\binom{V}{k}}\,\prod_{P\in\Pi}\,\prod_{v\in P\cap X}\alpha n\binom{n}{\alpha n}

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{n}{k}\left(\alpha n\binom{n}{\alpha n}\right)^{k}\leqslant 2^{H(\alpha)kn+2k\log_{2}n+k\log_{2}\alpha}\leqslant 2^{2H(\alpha)kn}\,,

where we used that
\binom{a}{b}\leqslant\binom{xa}{xb}
for
x\geqslant 1
. We also trivially have

|{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{btw}}_{\Pi,l}|\leqslant\binom{\binom{n}{2}}{l}\leqslant\left(\frac{en^{2}}{2l}\right)^{l}\,.

Let
\xi,\zeta>0
be the constants from
Lemma

5.4
, and let
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small such that
H(\alpha)<\xi/6
. Then for sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}}|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=2}^{n}~\sum_{l=1}^{\epsilon n^{2}}~\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi,k,l}}|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=2}^{n}~\sum_{l=1}^{\epsilon n^{2}}~\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi,k,l}}\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})-e(T[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}])}e^{-\xi kn-\zeta n\cdot\min\{l,n\}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{k=2}^{n}~\sum_{l=1}^{\epsilon n^{2}}|{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{cb}}_{\Pi,k}|\cdot|{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{btw}}_{\Pi,l}|\cdot e^{-\xi kn-\zeta n\cdot\min\{l,n\}}\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{sp}}_{\Pi}}\binom{e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}})}{m-e(\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}^{c})-e(T[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}])}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|\cdot e^{-\xi n}\left(\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}2^{2H(\alpha)kn}e^{-\xi kn/2}\right)\left(\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\exp\left(l\log\left(\frac{en^{2}}{2l}\right)-\zeta n\cdot\min\{l,\,n\}\right)\right)

(24)

\displaystyle\leqslant e^{-\xi n/2}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|\,,

where we again used
Corollary

C.3.1
to deduce the third inequality, and the last inequality holds since
H(\alpha)\to 0
as
\epsilon\to 0
and the two rightmost factors on the left-hand side of
(
24
)
are geometric series.

Let
\nu>0
be the constant from
Lemma

5.5
. Since for all
\Pi^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{B}}
, there are at most
\binom{n}{s}2^{s}
divisions
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}
such that
\Pi_{\ast}=\Pi^{\prime}
, we use
Lemma

5.5
to deduce

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{\delta n}\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{s=1}^{\delta n}\binom{e(\Pi_{\ast})}{m-e(\Pi_{\ast}^{c})}e^{-\nu sn}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{B}}}\binom{e(\Pi)}{m-e(\Pi^{c})}\sum_{s=1}^{\delta n}\binom{n}{s}2^{s}e^{-\nu sn}

(25)

\displaystyle\leqslant e^{-\nu n/2}\left(\sum_{s=1}^{\infty}e^{-\nu sn/2+s\log n+(\log 2)s}\right)\cdot 2|{\mathcal{B}}_{c}(n,m)|\leqslant e^{-\nu n/4}|{\mathcal{B}}_{c}(n,m)|\,,

where the third inequality follows from
Corollary

C.3.1
, and the last inequality follows by evaluating a geometric series.

Using
(
23
)
,
(
24
)
, and
(
25
)
, we have

(26)

\begin{split}\sum_{s=1}^{\delta n}\,\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}}\Bigg(|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|+\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}}|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}|+|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}|\Bigg)&\leqslant\sum_{s=1}^{\delta n}\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}}\big(1+e^{-\xi n/2}+e^{-\beta n^{2}/2}\big)|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|\\
&\leqslant 2e^{-\nu n/4}|{\mathcal{B}}_{c}(n,m)|\,,\end{split}

and similarly

(27)

\begin{split}\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{0}}\Bigg(\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}}|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}|+|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}|\Bigg)\leqslant\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{0}}(e^{-\xi n/2}+e^{-\beta n^{2}/2})|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|\leqslant 2e^{-\xi n/2}|{\mathcal{B}}_{c}(n,m)|\,.\end{split}

Let
C>0
be the constant from
Proposition

3.8
. By combining
(
21
)
,
(
26
)
, and
(
27
)
, we obtain

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)|

\displaystyle\leqslant|{\mathcal{B}}_{c}(n,m)|+\sum_{s=1}^{\delta n}\,\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|+\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}}\Bigg(\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}}|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Pi,T}|+|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{\Pi}|\Bigg)+|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathsf{far}}|

\displaystyle\leqslant(1+2e^{-\nu n/4}+2e^{-\xi n/2})|{\mathcal{B}}_{c}(n,m)|+e^{-Cn^{2}}|{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)|\,,

completing the proof.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

1.5

2
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}:=\bigcup_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}
. For a fixed set
S\subseteq V
of size
|S|\leqslant\delta n/2
,
Lemma

C.2
proves that almost all
G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}
with
\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}=S
have the property that
\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}(G)=\{A,B\}
is almost equitable (that is,
||A|-|B||\leqslant\sqrt{\log n}
). Hence in the remainder, we assume
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}
has the property that
\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}
is almost equitable.

Let
{\mathscr{P}}^{(1)}
denote the set of all
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}
such that
|\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}|\leqslant\log n
, and let
{\mathscr{P}}^{(2)}:={\mathscr{P}}\setminus{\mathscr{P}}^{(1)}
. Let
s\geqslant 1
,
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}
, and
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{\Pi}
. Denote
\Pi_{\mathsf{cb}}=\{A,B\}
and let
a:=|A|
and
b:=|B|
. Let
s_{a},s_{b}\geqslant 0
be integers such that
s_{a}+s_{b}=s
and
|a+s_{a}-b-s_{b}|
is minimum. (Notice
s_{a}
and
s_{b}
are uniquely determined by
\Pi
.) Fix a bipartition
\Pi_{\ast}=\{A_{\ast},B_{\ast}\}
of
V
such that
A\subseteq A_{\ast}
,
B\subseteq B_{\ast}
,
|A_{\ast}|=a+s_{a}
, and
|B_{\ast}|=b+s_{b}
. Define the quantities

\begin{array}[]{ll}N:=(a+s_{a})(b+s_{b})\,,&\displaystyle M:=m-\binom{a+s_{a}}{2}-\binom{b+s_{b}}{2}\,,\\[15.0pt]
K:=s_{b}a+s_{a}b\,,&\displaystyle L:=s_{a}a+s_{b}b+\binom{s_{a}}{2}+\binom{s_{b}}{2}-e(T)\,.\end{array}

Since
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}
,
Lemma

5.1
proves
|a-n/2|\leqslant\delta n
and
|b-n/2|\leqslant\delta n
. Using
Lemma

C.3
and a second-order series expansion, we compute

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi,T}|

\displaystyle\sim\binom{ab}{m-\binom{a}{2}-\binom{b}{2}-e(T)}=\binom{N-K}{M+L}

\displaystyle=\binom{N}{M}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{K+L}(N-M-i+1)}{\big(\prod_{i=1}^{L}(M+i)\big)\big(\prod_{i=1}^{K}(N-i+1)\big)}

\displaystyle=\binom{N}{M}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{K+L}(1-\frac{i-1}{N-M})}{\big(\prod_{i=1}^{K}(\frac{N}{N-M}-\frac{i-1}{N-M})\big)\big(\prod_{i=1}^{L}(\frac{M}{N-M}+\frac{i}{N-M})\big)}

\displaystyle=\binom{N}{M}\left(\frac{N-M}{N}\right)^{K}\left(\frac{N-M}{M}\right)^{L}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{(K+L)^{2}}{2(N-M)}+\frac{K^{2}}{2N}-\frac{L^{2}}{2M}+O\left(\frac{(K+L)^{3}}{(N-M)^{2}}\right)\right)

(28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=\binom{N}{M}\left(\frac{(N-M)^{2}}{NM}\right)^{L}\left(\frac{N-M}{N}\right)^{K-L}\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{(K+L)^{2}}{2(N-M)}+\frac{K^{2}}{2N}-\frac{L^{2}}{2M}+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\right)\,.\end{split}

Notice that for sufficiently large
n
, we have
L\geqslant sn/2
,
M\leqslant\frac{1}{2}(\gamma-1/2)n^{2}
, and
a+b\geqslant(1-4\delta)n
. Hence we compute that

\displaystyle\frac{(K+L)^{2}}{2(N-M)}-\frac{K^{2}}{2N}+\frac{L^{2}}{2M}

\displaystyle\geqslant L\left(\frac{K+L}{N-M}+\frac{L}{2M}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{sn}{2}\left(\frac{s(a+b)+\binom{s_{a}}{2}+\binom{s_{b}}{2}-e(T)}{\binom{n}{2}-m}+\frac{s}{(2\gamma-1)n}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{(1-4\delta)s^{2}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{s^{2}}{4\gamma-2}\geqslant 6s^{2}\,.

We now claim
((N-M)^{2}/(NM))^{L}|=O(n)
. Indeed, since
|a-b|\leqslant\sqrt{\log n}
, we have

\left|(1-\rho)-\frac{N-M}{N}\right|=O\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)\hskip 17.07164pt\text{and}\hskip 17.07164pt\left|\frac{1-\rho}{\rho}-\frac{N-M}{M}\right|=O\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)\,,

which implies (using that
(1-\rho)^{2}/\rho=1
)

\left|1-\frac{(N-M)^{2}}{NM}\right|=O\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)\,.

The fact that
L=O(n)
now completes the proof of our claim.

Now if
\Pi
satisfies
|\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}|=s\geqslant\log n
then from
(
28
)
,

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi,T}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{N}{M}\left(\frac{(N-M)^{2}}{NM}\right)^{L}\left(\frac{N-M}{N}\right)^{(s_{b}-s_{a})(a-b)-\binom{s_{a}}{2}-\binom{s_{b}}{2}+e(T)}e^{-5s^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{N}{M}\left(\frac{N-M}{N}\right)^{-\binom{s_{a}}{2}-\binom{s_{b}}{2}}e^{-4s^{2}}\leqslant\binom{N}{M}e^{-2s^{2}}\,,

where we used that
(s_{b}-s_{a})(a-b)\geqslant 0
and
1<N/(N-M)<e
. Since for all
s\geqslant 0
and
\Pi^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{P}}
, there are at most
\binom{n}{s}2^{s}
divisions
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}
such that
\Pi_{\ast}=\Pi^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}^{(2)}}\,\sum_{T\in{\mathcal{T}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{\Pi,T}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{s\geqslant\log n}\,\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}}\binom{N}{M}e^{-\frac{3}{2}s^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{s\geqslant\log n}\binom{n}{s}2^{s}e^{-\frac{3}{2}s^{2}}\sum_{\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}}\binom{e(\Pi)}{m-e(\Pi^{c})}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\cdot|{\mathcal{B}}_{c}(n,m)|\sum_{s\geqslant\log n}e^{-\frac{3}{2}s^{2}+s\log n+(\log 2)n}\leqslant\frac{n^{-\log n}}{\log n}\cdot|{\mathcal{B}}(n,m)|\,,

where we used that
|{\mathcal{T}}^{\ast}_{\Pi}|\leqslant e^{s^{2}/2}
for
\Pi\in{\mathscr{P}}_{s}
, and we used a standard Gaussian tail bound to obtain the last inequality. Combining the previous bound with the rest of the proof of
Theorem

1.5
immediately implies the result.
∎

6.
The Subcritical Regime

In this section we prove the third assertion of
Theorem

1.5
. Throughout the section, let
\gamma\in\big(0,\frac{5-\sqrt{5}}{4}\big)
and
m\sim\gamma\binom{n}{2}
. There is a global parameter
\epsilon>0
used throughout the section; in general, all statements hold for sufficiently small
\epsilon
, but in some cases it will be useful to have established that
0<\epsilon<\min\{\gamma,\frac{5-\sqrt{5}}{4}-\gamma\}/2^{65}
. Likewise, inequalities
f(n)\leqslant g(n)
are always meant for large enough
n
.

6.1.
Parameters and Definitions

Define the parameters and constants

\begin{array}[]{llll}\displaystyle\tau:=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2^{8}}\right)^{67}\,,&\delta:=8\sqrt{\epsilon}\,,&\alpha:=8\epsilon^{1/8}\,,&\eta:=\epsilon^{1/16}\,,\\[10.0pt]
\omega:=\epsilon^{1/32}\,,&\displaystyle\mu:=\bigg(\frac{5+\sqrt{5}}{20}\gamma\bigg)^{1/2}\,,&\displaystyle\rho:=\frac{3-\sqrt{5}}{2}\,.\end{array}

Let
\bm{\lambda}_{\ast}:=(0,2\mu)
and
W^{\ast}:=W_{\bm{\lambda}_{\ast}}
(using the definition in
(
7
)
), so
W^{\ast}
is the unique optimal graphon
W_{\bm{\lambda}}\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\ast}_{\gamma}
(up to equivalence) of the variational problem
(
6
)
satisfying
|\bm{\lambda}|=2
.

Let
A_{1},B_{1},\dots,A_{\ell},B_{\ell}\subseteq V
be disjoint nonempty vertex subsets, let
\Pi_{i}:=\{A_{i},B_{i}\}
for all
i\in[\ell]
, and let
\Pi:=\{\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\ell}\}
; we call such a set
\Pi
an
division
of
V
. Let
{\mathscr{D}}
denote the set of all divisions of
V
. We will always assume the
\Pi_{i}
are ordered such that the numbers
|A_{i}|+|B_{i}|
are nonincreasing in
i
. When there is no ambiguity, we use the notation
\Pi_{i}
to refer to the complete bipartite graph
K_{\Pi_{i}}
, and
\Pi
to refer to the vertex-disjoint union of bipartite graphs
\Pi_{i}
. For all divisions
\Pi\in{\mathscr{D}}
, let
V(\Pi):=\bigcup\bigcup\Pi\subseteq V
be the vertex set covered by
\Pi
, let
v(\Pi):=|V(\Pi)|
, and let
\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}:=V\setminus V(\Pi)
.

Let
\Pi=\{\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\ell}\}\in{\mathscr{D}}
be a division and denote
\Pi_{i}=\{A_{i},B_{i}\}
for all
i\in[\ell]
. Also let
v_{i}:=|A_{i}\cup B_{i}|
for all
i\in[\ell]
. Let
\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell]
be the greatest index such that
v_{\ell^{\prime}}\geqslant 8\delta n
. Let
\bm{\lambda}=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\dots)\in\Lambda
(see
Section

3
for the definition of
\Lambda
) and let
W:=W_{\bm{\lambda}}
. Denote
\mu_{i}:=\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i-1}
for all
i\geqslant 1
. Let
L
be the set
{\mathbb{N}}
if
|\bm{\lambda}|=\infty
and
\{1,\dots,|\bm{\lambda}|\}
otherwise. Define the set of divisions

{\mathscr{D}}_{W}:=\left\{\Pi\in{\mathscr{D}}:\begin{multlined}\vphantom{\displaystyle\bigcup}\text{there exists an injection $\varphi:[\ell^{\prime}]\to L$ such that}\\
\\
\text{$|v_{i}-\mu_{\varphi(i)}n|\leqslant\delta n$ for all $i\in[\ell^{\prime}]$, and $\mu_{i}\leqslant\delta$ for all $i\in L\setminus\varphi([\ell^{\prime}])$}\end{multlined}\right\}\,.

Since for every graphon
U\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\ast}_{\gamma}
there is a unique sequence
\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda
such that
U
is equivalent to
W_{\bm{\lambda}}
, the notation
{\mathscr{D}}_{U}:={\mathscr{D}}_{W_{\bm{\lambda}}}
is well-defined.

For all graphs
G
and divisions
\Pi=\{\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\ell}\}
with
\Pi_{i}=\{A_{i},B_{i}\}
, let

\displaystyle b(G,\Pi)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{P\in\Pi_{i}}e(G^{c}[P])+\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant\ell}e(G[A_{i}\cup B_{i},A_{j}\cup B_{j}])+e(G[V,\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}])\,.

Let
\Pi(G)\in{\mathscr{D}}
denote a canonically chosen division minimizing
b(G,\Pi)
, and define
b(G):=b(G,\Pi(G))
. For all
G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m)
with
\Pi(G)=\{\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\ell}\}
,
\Pi_{i}=\{A_{i},B_{i}\}
, define

D(G):=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\bigcup_{P\in\Pi_{i}}E(G^{c}[P])\cup\bigcup_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant\ell}E(G[A_{i}\cup B_{i},A_{j}\cup B_{j}])\cup E(G[V(\Pi),\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}])\,.

Let
U(G):=V(D(G))
denote the set of vertices incident to
D(G)
and define the graph
T(G):=(U(G),D(G))
. For all
W\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\ast}_{\gamma}
and
\Pi\in{\mathscr{D}}_{W}
, define

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{C}}_{W,\Pi}&:=\left\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m):\begin{multlined}\text{$\delta_{\square}(G,W)<\tau$, $\Pi(G)=\Pi$, and $\exists~\Pi^{\prime}\in\Pi$}\vphantom{\displaystyle\bigcup}\\
\\
\text{such that $|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant\eta n$ and $e_{T(G)}(V,V(\Pi^{\prime}))\geqslant 1$}\end{multlined}\right\}\,,\\[20.0pt]
{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{W,\Pi}&:=\left\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m):\begin{multlined}\text{$\delta_{\square}(G,W)<\tau$, $\Pi(G)=\Pi$, and $\forall~\Pi^{\prime}\in\Pi$,}\vphantom{\displaystyle\bigcup}\\
\\
\text{$|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant\eta n$ implies $e_{T(G)}(V,V(\Pi^{\prime}))=0$}\end{multlined}\right\}\,,\end{array}

and define the associated sets of graphs

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}&:=\{T(G)\cup G[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}]:G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{W,\Pi}\}\,,\\[5.0pt]
{\mathcal{T}}^{\ast}_{W,\Pi}&:=\{G[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}]:G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{W,\Pi}\}\,.\end{array}

For all
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}
and
P\in\Pi^{\prime}\in\Pi
, where
|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant\eta n
, say that a vertex
v\in V
has

(1)

low degree in
P
if
d_{T}(v,P)<\alpha|P|
,

(2)

medium degree in
P
if
\alpha|P|\leqslant d_{T}(v,P)\leqslant(1-\alpha)|P|
, or

(3)

high degree in
P
if
d_{T}(v,P)>(1-\alpha)|P|
.

If a vertex
v
has medium degree in some
P\in\Pi^{\prime}\in\Pi
with respect to
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}
, we say that
v
has
(P,T)
-medium degree. Define the sets

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi}&:=\left\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{W,\Pi}:\begin{multlined}\text{for some $\Pi^{\prime}=\{A,B\}\in\Pi$ and $v\in V\setminus V(\Pi^{\prime})$,}\vphantom{\displaystyle\bigcup}\\
\\
\text{$|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant\eta n$, $d_{T(G)}(v,A)>(1-\alpha)|A|$, and $d_{T(G)}(v,B)\geqslant\alpha|B|$}\end{multlined}\right\}\,,\\[20.0pt]
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi}&:=\left\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{W,\Pi}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi}:\begin{multlined}\text{for some $\Pi^{\prime}\in\Pi$, $P\in\Pi^{\prime}$, and $v\in V$,}\vphantom{\displaystyle\bigcup}\\
\\
\text{$|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant\eta n$ and $v$ has $(P,T(G))$-medium degree}\end{multlined}\right\}\,,\end{array}

and for all
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}
define the set

{\mathcal{C}}_{W,\Pi,T}:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}_{W,\Pi}\setminus({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi}\cup{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi}):T(G)\cup G[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}]=T\}\,.

For all
n,m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\tau>0
, and graphons
W\in{\mathcal{W}}
, define the neighborhood

N_{\tau}(n,m,W):=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}(n,m):\delta_{\square}(G,W)<\tau\}\,.

In the following lemma, recall that the sets
\Lambda
and
{\mathcal{V}}
were defined in
Section

3
, and recall the discretization
W_{n}
of a graphon
W
from
Definition

5.2
.

Lemma 6.1
.

If
W\in{\mathcal{V}}
and
G\in N_{\tau}(n,m,W)
then
\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(G,W_{n})<\epsilon^{2}/8
for large enough
n
. Additionally, by editing (i.e. adding or removing) at most
\epsilon n^{2}
edges of
G
, we can obtain a graph that is the vertex-disjoint union of co-bipartite graphs and an independent set.

Proof.

We may assume
W=W_{\bm{\lambda}}
, where
\bm{\lambda}=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\dots)\in\Lambda
. Let
\mu_{i}:=\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i-1}
for
i\geqslant 1
. For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, let
H_{n}
be the weighted graph on
V
whose
ij
edge weight is
W(\frac{i}{n},\frac{j}{n})
with unweighted nodes. In the remainder, all statements hold for large enough
n
. The sequence
W_{n}=W_{H_{n}}
clearly converges to
W
pointwise almost everywhere and hence in cut metric, so the condition
\delta_{\square}(G,W)<\tau
implies
\delta_{\square}(G,W_{n})<2\tau
. By
[
41
, Lemma 8.9]
we have
\delta_{\square}(G,H_{n})=\delta_{\square}(W_{G},W_{n})
, and by
[
15
, Theorem 2.3]
we have

\widehat{\delta}(G_{1},G_{2})\leqslant 32\cdot(\delta_{\square}(G_{1},G_{2}))^{1/67}

for edge-weighted graphs with the same number of unweighted nodes; from these two results we deduce that
\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(G,H_{n})<\epsilon^{2}/8
.

Let
0\leqslant k\leqslant 1/\epsilon
be the greatest index such that
\mu_{k}\geqslant\epsilon
. Let
G_{0}
be an isomorphic copy of
G
such that
d_{\square}(G_{0},H_{n})=\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(G,H_{n})
, and let
\phi:V(G)\to V(G_{0})
be the isomorphism. For all
i\in[k]
, define the sets

\begin{array}[]{ll}C_{i,1}&:=\displaystyle\left\{j\in[n]:j/n\in\left(\lambda_{i-1}\,,\,\frac{\lambda_{i-1}+\lambda_{i}}{2}\right)\right\}\,,\\[12.0pt]
C_{i,2}&:=\displaystyle\left\{j\in[n]:j/n\in\left(\frac{\lambda_{i-1}+\lambda_{i}}{2}\,,\,\lambda_{i}\right)\right\}\,,\\[12.0pt]
C_{i}&:=C_{i,1}\cup C_{i,2}\,,\end{array}

and let
J:=V\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}C_{i}
. Since
d_{\square}(G_{0},H_{n})<\epsilon^{2}/8
, we have
e(G_{0}^{c}[C_{i,j}])\leqslant\epsilon^{2}n^{2}/8
for all
i\in[k]
and
j\in[2]
. Similarly, for all distinct
i,j\in[k]
, we have
e(G_{0}[C_{i},C_{j}])\leqslant\epsilon^{2}n^{2}/8
. Notice also that there are at most
(k+1)n
edges in
G
incident to vertices
i\in[n]
that satisfy
i/n=\lambda_{j}
for some
j\in[k]
; let
U
denote the set of these vertices.

Let
\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}:=(\lambda_{0},\dots,\lambda_{k})
and let
H^{\prime}_{n}
be the weighted graph on
V
whose
ij
edge weight is
W_{\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}}(\frac{i}{n},\frac{j}{n})
with unweighted nodes. Since the sequence
(W_{\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}})_{n}=W_{H^{\prime}_{n}}
of graphons converges to
W_{\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}}
pointwise almost everywhere, we have

\displaystyle e(G_{0}[J])

\displaystyle\leqslant d_{\square}(G_{0},H^{\prime}_{n})

\displaystyle\leqslant d_{\square}(G_{0},H_{n})+d_{\square}(H_{n},H^{\prime}_{n})

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{8}+2\int_{[\lambda_{k},1]^{2}}W(x,y)\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{8}+2\sum_{k\leqslant i<|\bm{\lambda}|}\frac{\mu_{i}^{2}}{2}\Bigg(1+\frac{3-\sqrt{5}}{2}\Bigg)\leqslant\frac{\epsilon}{4}\,,

where the last inequality holds since
\mu_{i}<\epsilon
for all
i>k
. From the above considerations, it follows immediately that by adding at most
\epsilon n^{2}/4
edges to
G_{0}
in the vertex sets
C_{i,j}
, removing at most
\epsilon n^{2}/8
edges from
G_{0}
in the edge sets
E(G_{0}[C_{i},C_{j}])
, removing the edges incident to
U
, and removing at most
\epsilon n^{2}/4
edges from
G_{0}
in the vertex set
J
, we can obtain a graph that is the vertex-disjoint union of co-bipartite graphs (on the vertex sets
C_{i}
) and an independent set (on the vertex set
J\cup U
). Since we made at most
\epsilon n^{2}
edits (provided
n
is large enough that
(k+1)n\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}/4
), this completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
W\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\ast}_{\gamma}
. For sufficiently large
n
, all graphs
G\in N_{\tau}(n,m,W)
satisfy the following conditions:

(1)

\Pi(G)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{W}
.

(2)

For all
\Pi^{\prime}=\{A,B\}\in\Pi(G)
such that
|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant\alpha n
, it holds that

\left(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha\right)|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\leqslant\min\{|A|,\,|B|\}\leqslant\max\{|A|,\,|B|\}\leqslant\left(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha\right)|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\,.

(3)

For all
\Pi^{\prime}=\{A,B\}\in\Pi(G)
such that
|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant\delta n
, we have
|\rho-d_{G}(A,B)|\leqslant\delta
.

(4)

For all
\Pi^{\prime}=\{A,B\}\in\Pi(G)
such that
|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant 8\delta n
and all vertices
v\in A
, it holds that
d_{T(G)}(v,A)\leqslant(1-\alpha)|A|
.

Proof.

Denote
\Pi:=\Pi(G)=\{\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{r}\}
,
\Pi_{j}=\{A_{j},B_{j}\}
, and
V_{j}=A_{j}\cup B_{j}
. Let
r^{\prime}\in[r]
be the greatest index such that
|V_{r^{\prime}}|\geqslant 8\delta n
. We may assume
W=W_{\bm{\lambda}}
, where
\bm{\lambda}=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\dots)\in\Lambda
. Let
\mu_{i}:=\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i-1}
for
i\geqslant 1
, and let
1\leqslant k\leqslant 1/\delta
be the greatest index such that
\mu_{k}\geqslant\delta
. For
i\geqslant 1
, let
C_{i}
,
C_{i,1}
, and
C_{i,2}
be the vertex sets defined in the proof of
Lemma

6.1
. Notice that
|C_{i}-\mu_{i}n|\leqslant 2
and
|C_{i,j}-\mu_{i}n/2|\leqslant 2
. To prove the first assertion of the lemma, it suffices to establish an injection
\varphi:[r^{\prime}]\to[k]
satisfying the conditions in the definition of
{\mathscr{D}}_{W}
.

Let
H_{n}
be the same weighted graph as in the proof of
Lemma

6.1
and assume the vertices of
G
are labeled such that
d_{\square}(G,H_{n})=\widehat{\delta}_{\square}(G,H_{n})
. From the proof of
Lemma

6.1
, we know
d_{\square}(G,H_{n})\leqslant\epsilon^{2}/8
. Let
H
be the graph on the vertex set
V
and edge set
(E(G)\,\Delta\,D(G))\setminus E(G[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}])
, where
\Delta
is the symmetric difference (so
H
is the graph that witnesses the fact that
\Pi
is optimal for
G
). By
Lemma

6.1
, we know
d_{\square}(G,H)n^{2}\leqslant d_{1}(G,H)n^{2}=b(G,\Pi)\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}
(where
d_{1}
denotes the normalized edit distance), hence

(29)

d_{\square}(H,H_{n})\leqslant d_{\square}(H,G)+d_{\square}(G,H_{n})\leqslant 2\epsilon\,.

By definition, for all
i\geqslant 1
and
j=1,2
, the weighted graph
H_{n}[C_{i,j}]
is a clique (i.e. has all edge-weights equal to 1). Since
H
is a disjoint union of co-bipartite graphs and an independent set, the following holds for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant k
and
j=1,2
: there is a subset
C^{\prime}_{i,j}\subseteq C_{i,j}
of size at least
|C_{i,j}|-\ell
such that
H[C^{\prime}_{i,j}]
is a clique, where
\ell
is the greatest integer satisfying the inequality
\ell\cdot\delta n+\binom{\ell}{2}\leqslant 2\epsilon n^{2}
; it is easy to check that this
\ell
satisfies
\ell\leqslant\delta n/32
.

For the remainder of the proof fix
1\leqslant i\leqslant k
. Note that since
H[C^{\prime}_{i,1}]
is a clique and
H
is a vertex-disjoint union of co-bipartite graphs and an independent set, we must have
C^{\prime}_{i,1}\subseteq V_{s}
for some
s\in[r]
, and we must then also have
C^{\prime}_{i,2}\subseteq V_{s}
. We claim that

(30)

|A_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,1}|\leqslant\delta n/16\hskip 19.91692pt\text{or}\hskip 19.91692pt|A_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,2}|\leqslant\delta n/16\,.

Assume to the contrary that neither inequality in
(
30
)
held. Now
H[A_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,1},A_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,2}]
is a complete bipartite graph since it is a subgraph of
H[A_{s}]
, but the edge-weights of
H_{n}[A_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,1},A_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,2}]
all equal
\rho
, which implies
d_{\square}(H,H_{n})\geqslant 16\epsilon(1-\rho)n^{2}>2\epsilon n^{2}
. This contradicts
(
29
)
, hence
(
30
)
must hold. Since there is symmetry in
A_{s}
and
B_{s}
,
(
30
)
also holds with
A_{s}
replaced by
B_{s}
.

Define the sets
U_{1}:=B_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,1}
and
U_{2}:=A_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,2}
. We claim
|U_{1}|\leqslant\delta n/16
and
|U_{2}|\leqslant\delta n/16
(possibly after swapping the roles of
A_{s}
and
B_{s}
). Due to
(
30
)
and symmetry in
A_{s}
and
B_{s}
, we may assume at least one of the two claimed inequalities holds. Assume the second claimed inequality holds but not the first. Now
H[U_{1},B_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,2}]
is a complete bipartite graph since it is a subgraph of
H[B_{s}]
, but the edge-weights of the induced bipartite graph
H_{n}[U_{1},B_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,2}]
all equal
\rho
, which implies
d_{\square}(H,H_{n})>2\epsilon
, contradicting
(
29
)
. Hence both claimed inequalities hold.

Define the sets
U^{\prime}_{1}:=A_{s}\setminus C_{i}
and
U^{\prime}_{2}:=B_{s}\setminus C_{i}
. We now claim
|U^{\prime}_{1}|\leqslant\delta n/16
and
|U^{\prime}_{2}|\leqslant\delta n/16
. Assume the first claimed inequality does not hold. Now
H[U^{\prime}_{1},C^{\prime}_{i,1}]
is a complete bipartite graph, but the edge-weights of the induced bipartite graph
H_{n}[U^{\prime}_{1},C^{\prime}_{i,1}]
all equal zero, which implies
d_{\square}(H,H_{n})>2\epsilon
, contradicting
(
29
)
. By symmetry in
A_{s}
and
B_{s}
, both claimed inequalities hold.

Combining the conclusions of the previous three paragraphs, we have

\displaystyle|A_{s}\,\Delta\,C_{i,1}|

\displaystyle=|A_{s}\setminus C_{i,1}|+|C_{i,1}\setminus A_{s}|

\displaystyle=|A_{s}\setminus C_{i}|+|A_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,2}|+|A_{s}\cap(C_{i,2}\setminus C^{\prime}_{i,2})|+|B_{s}\cap C^{\prime}_{i,1}|+|C_{i,1}\setminus C^{\prime}_{i,1}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta n/16+\delta n/16+\delta n/32+\delta n/16+\delta n/32\leqslant\delta n/4\,,

and by symmetry in
A_{s}
and
B_{s}
we have
|B_{s}\,\Delta\,C_{i,2}|\leqslant\delta n/4
. It follows that

(31)

|(A_{s}\cup B_{s})\,\Delta\,C_{i}|\leqslant|A_{s}\,\Delta\,C_{i,1}|+|B_{s}\,\Delta\,C_{i,2}|\leqslant\delta n/2\,.

We have thus established an injection
f:[k]\to[r]
such that
|(A_{f(i)}\cup B_{f(i)})\,\Delta\,C_{i}|\leqslant\delta n/2
for all
i\in[k]
. Notice also that
f
maps onto
[r^{\prime}]
since if there existed
s\in[r^{\prime}]\setminus f([k])
, then we would have
d_{\square}(H,H_{n})\geqslant 2\epsilon
(since
\Pi_{s}
cannot satisfy a condition similar to
(
31
)
for any
C_{i}
). It follows that
\varphi:[r^{\prime}]\to[k]
defined by
\varphi(s)=f^{-1}(s)
is the claimed injection.

To prove
2
, notice that
||C_{i,1}|-|C_{i,2}||\leqslant 2
, so the inequalities
|A_{s}\,\Delta\,C_{i,1}|\leqslant\delta n/4
and
|B_{s}\,\Delta\,C_{i,2}|\leqslant\delta n/4
imply
||A_{s}|-|B_{s}||\leqslant\delta n/2+2
. Using this last inequality, the conclusion of
2
follows easily whenever
|V_{s}|\geqslant\alpha n
.

To prove
3
, we use all the same definitions and conclusions as in the first part of the lemma. If we define the sets
D_{1}:=A_{s}\cap C_{i,1}
and
D_{2}:=B_{s}\cap C_{i,2}
, since the edge-weights of the induced bipartite graph
H_{n}[D_{1},D_{2}]
all equal
\rho
, we know
|e_{G}(D_{1},D_{2})-\rho|D_{1}|\cdot|D_{2}||\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}
. We compute that

\displaystyle d_{G}(A_{s},B_{s})

\displaystyle=\frac{e_{G}(A_{s}\cap C_{i,1},B_{s}\cap C_{i,2})+e_{G}(A_{s}\setminus C_{i,1},B_{s})+e_{G}(A_{s},B_{s}\setminus C_{i,2})}{|A_{s}|\cdot|B_{s}|}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\rho|D_{1}|\cdot|D_{2}|+\epsilon n^{2}+|A_{s}\,\Delta\,C_{i,1}|\cdot n+|B_{s}\,\Delta\,C_{i,2}|\cdot n}{|A_{s}|\cdot|B_{s}|}

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho+\epsilon+\delta n^{2}/2\leqslant\rho+\delta\,,

and a similar calculation shows
d_{G}(A_{s},B_{s})\geqslant\rho-\delta
.

To prove
4
, let
\Pi^{\prime}=\{A,B\}\in\Pi
and suppose
d_{T(G)}(v,A)>(1-\alpha)|A|
. By the optimality of
\Pi(G)
, we know
\overline{d}_{G}(v,B)\geqslant\overline{d}_{G}(v,A)
. If
\Pi^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathscr{D}}
is the division obtained by moving
v
from
\Pi^{\prime}
to
\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}
, then using the fact that
||A|-|B||\leqslant 2\delta n
(proved in
1
),

\displaystyle b(G,\Pi^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=b(G,\Pi)+d_{G}(v,A)+d_{G}(v,B)-d_{T(G)}(v,A)

\displaystyle\leqslant b(G,\Pi)+\alpha|A|+(\alpha|A|+|B|-|A|)-(1-\alpha)|A|

\displaystyle\leqslant b(G,\Pi)-(1-3\alpha)\cdot 8\delta n+2\delta n<b(G,\Pi)\,,

which contradicts the optimality of
\Pi=\Pi(G)
, completing the proof.
∎

For all
\Pi=\{\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\ell}\}\in{\mathscr{D}}
with
\Pi_{i}=\{A_{i},B_{i}\}
and all
0\leqslant t\leqslant\binom{n}{2}
, define the set

{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}:=\left\{(m_{1},\dots,m_{\ell})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\ell}:\begin{multlined}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}(e(\Pi_{i}^{c})+m_{i})=m-t\text{ and}\vphantom{\displaystyle\bigcup}\\
\\
\vphantom{\displaystyle\bigcup}\text{for all $i\in[\ell]$ s.t. $|V(\Pi_{i})|\geqslant\delta n$, $\rho-\delta\leqslant\frac{m_{i}}{|A_{i}|\cdot|B_{i}|}\leqslant\rho+\delta$}\end{multlined}\right\}\,.

Lemma

6.2
proves that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}
contains the sequences of numbers describing the possible number of edges between parts in optimal divisions
\Pi(G)
of graphs
G\in N_{\tau}(n,m,W)
. On several occasions we will use the fact that for all
W\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\ast}_{\gamma}
and
\Pi\in{\mathscr{D}}_{W}
,

(32)

\max_{0\leqslant t\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\binom{e(\Pi_{i})}{m_{i}}\leqslant 2\cdot|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{W,\Pi}|\,,

which follows from the fact that for all
\bm{m}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}
, almost all graphs
G[V(\Pi_{i})]
such that
G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{W,\Pi}
and
e_{G}(\Pi_{i})=m_{i}
admit a unique 2-clique-cover, as proven in
Corollary

C.3.1
.

Definition 6.3

(The random graph
G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}
)
.

Let
\Pi=\{\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\ell}\}\in{\mathscr{D}}
and
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}
. Let
0\leqslant t\leqslant m
and let
\bm{m}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}
. For all
i\in[\ell]
, let
H_{i}\subseteq\Pi_{i}
be the uniformly random bipartite graph with
m_{i}
edges (where the graphs
H_{1},\dots,H_{\ell}
are independent). Let
G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}
denote the random graph on the vertex set
V
and edge set

(33)

E(G)=\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}(E(\Pi_{i}^{c})\cup E(H_{i}))\right)\Delta\,E(T)\,,

where
\Delta
denotes the symmetric difference.

Lemma 6.4
.

For all
W\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\ast}_{\gamma}
and
\Pi\in{\mathscr{D}}_{W}
,

|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi}|\leqslant e^{-\beta n^{2}}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{W,\Pi}|\,,

where
\beta=\beta(\epsilon)>0
is a constant.

Proof.

Denote
\Pi=\{\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\ell}\}
,
\Pi_{i}=\{A_{i},B_{i}\}
, and
V_{i}=A_{i}\cup B_{i}
for all
i\in[\ell]
. For all
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}
,
\Pi^{\prime}=\{A,B\}\in\Pi
such that
|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant\eta n
, and
v\in V\setminus V(\Pi^{\prime})
, define

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi,T}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi}:T(G)\cup G[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}]=T\}\,,\\[5.0pt]
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi,A,T,v}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi,T}:\text{$d_{T}(v,A)>(1-\alpha)|A|$ and $d_{T}(v,B)\geqslant\alpha|B|$}\}\,.\end{array}

Fix
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}
,
\Pi_{i}\in\Pi
, and
v\in V\setminus V_{i}
such that
|V_{i}|\geqslant\eta n
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi,A_{i},T,v}
is nonempty. Define the quantity

t:=\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant\ell}e_{T}(V_{i},V_{j})+e_{T}(V,\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}})-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}e_{T}(V_{i})\,,

let
\bm{m}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}
, and let
G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}
be the random graph from
Definition

6.3
. Every graph
G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi,P,T,v}
is of the form
(
33
)
for some
\bm{m}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}
and
H\subseteq\Pi
, hence

|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi,P,T,v}|\leqslant\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}}{\mathbb{P}}\{G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\binom{e(\Pi_{i})}{m_{i}}\,.

In the remainder we bound the probability that
G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}
is claw-free. The optimality of the division
\Pi(G)
implies that
v\in V_{j}
and
G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi,A_{i},T,v}
then
v\not\in\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}
and
|V_{j}|\geqslant\eta n/4
(since otherwise one could obtain a more optimal division by moving
v
to
V_{i}
). Hence
v\in A_{j}
for some
\Pi_{j}=\{A_{j},B_{j}\}
with
|V(\Pi_{j})|\geqslant\eta n/4
. Now by applying
Lemma

4.2
with variable assignments

\begin{array}[]{llll}\Pi_{1}\leftarrow\Pi_{i}&\Pi_{2}\leftarrow\Pi_{j}&n_{1}\leftarrow|V(\Pi_{i})|&n_{2}\leftarrow|V(\Pi_{j})|\\[10.0pt]
m_{1}\leftarrow m_{i}&m_{2}\leftarrow m_{j}&T\leftarrow T[V_{i}\cup V_{j}]&v\leftarrow v\,,\end{array}

we directly obtain

{\mathbb{P}}\{G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\leqslant\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha^{2}\eta p_{1}p_{2}}{2^{33}}\cdot(\eta n)^{2}\right)\leqslant\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2^{30}}\cdot n^{2}\right)\,,

where we used that
n_{1}\geqslant\eta n
and
p_{1}p_{2}\geqslant 1/8
. We thus compute

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{t=1}^{\epsilon n^{2}}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}\\
e(T)=t\end{subarray}}\,\sum_{P\in\bigcup\Pi}\,\sum_{v\in V}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{W,\Pi,P,T,v}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}\cdot\binom{\binom{n}{2}}{\epsilon n^{2}}\cdot n^{2}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2^{30}}\cdot n^{2}\right)\max_{0\leqslant t\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\binom{e(\Pi_{i})}{m_{i}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\epsilon n^{4}\left(\frac{e}{2\epsilon}\right)^{\epsilon n^{2}}\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{\epsilon}}{2^{30}}\cdot n^{2}\right)\max_{0\leqslant t\leqslant\epsilon n^{2}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\binom{e(\Pi_{i})}{m_{i}}\leqslant e^{-\beta n^{2}}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{W,\Pi}|\,,

where we used
(
32
)
and the fact that
\sqrt{\epsilon}\gg\epsilon
as
\epsilon\to 0
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

For all
W\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\ast}_{\gamma}
and
\Pi\in{\mathscr{D}}_{W}
,

|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi}|\leqslant e^{-\beta n^{2}}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}_{W,\Pi}|\,,

where
\beta=\beta(\epsilon)>0
is a constant.

Proof.

Denote
\Pi=\{\Pi_{1},\dots,\Pi_{\ell}\}
,
\Pi_{i}=\{A_{i},B_{i}\}
, and
V_{i}=A_{i}\cup B_{i}
for all
i\in[\ell]
. For all
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}
,
\Pi^{\prime}\in\Pi
such that
|V(\Pi^{\prime})|\geqslant\eta n
,
P\in\Pi^{\prime}
, and
v\in V
, define the sets

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi,T}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi}:T(G)\cup G[\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}]=T\}\,,\\[5.0pt]
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi,P,T,v}&:=\{G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi,T}:v\text{ has $(P,T(G))$-medium degree}\}\,.\end{array}

Fix
T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}
,
\Pi_{i}\in\Pi
,
A\in\Pi_{i}
, and
v\in V
such that
|V_{i}|\geqslant\eta n
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi,A,T,v}
is nonempty. Let
B\in\Pi_{i}\setminus\{A\}
be the other part in
\Pi_{i}
. Define

t:=\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant\ell}e(T[V_{i},V_{j}])+e(T[V,\Pi_{\mathsf{sp}}])-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}e(T[V_{i}])\,,

let
\bm{m}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}
, and let
G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}
be the random graph from
Definition

6.3
. Every graph
G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi,P,T,v}
is of the form
(
33
)
for some
\bm{m}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}
and
H\subseteq\Pi
, hence

|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi,P,T,v}|\leqslant\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\Pi,t}}{\mathbb{P}}\{G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\binom{e(\Pi_{i})}{m_{i}}\,.

In the remainder we bound the probability that
G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}
is claw-free. We apply
Lemma

4.1
with the following variable assignments:

\begin{array}[]{llll}\Pi\leftarrow\Pi_{i}&V\leftarrow V_{i}&m\leftarrow m_{i}&v\leftarrow v\\[10.0pt]
N\leftarrow\overline{N}_{T}(v,A)&N^{\prime}\leftarrow\overline{N}_{T}(v,B)&T\leftarrow T[V_{i}]\,.\end{array}

Note that
\overline{N}_{T}(v,B)\geqslant\alpha|B|
by definition of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi}
, so the hypotheses of
Lemma

4.1
are indeed met, implying

{\mathbb{P}}\{G_{\Pi,T,\bm{m}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(n)\}\leqslant\exp\bigg(-\frac{\epsilon^{3/4}p}{2}(\eta n)^{2}\bigg)\leqslant\exp\bigg(-\frac{\epsilon^{7/8}}{8}n^{2}\bigg)\,,

where we used that
|V_{i}|\geqslant\eta n
and
p\geqslant 1/4
. We thus compute

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{t=1}^{\epsilon n^{2}}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W,\Pi}\\
e(T)=t\end{subarray}}\,\sum_{P\in\bigcup\Pi}\,\sum_{v\in V}|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{W,\Pi,P,T,v}|
